前言＋公民誓詞 / Introduction & Oath of Citizenship

對應 PDF 第 2–3 頁。對應 day-01.md。 排版：英文原文（引言區）→ 中文翻譯。

⚠️ 重要提醒

你下載的 PDF 是 2021 年版（ISBN 978-0-660-39273-8）。誓詞中仍是 Queen Elizabeth the Second。 2026 年的真實考試誓詞已改為 King Charles the Third（伊莉莎白二世於 2022 年 9 月過世）。 其餘內容（歷史、政府、地理、經濟）均仍適用。

Welcome / 歡迎詞

English

Welcome! It took courage to move to a new country. Your decision to apply for citizenship is another big step. You are becoming part of a great tradition that was built by generations of pioneers before you. Once you have met all the legal requirements, we hope to welcome you as a new citizen with all the rights and responsibilities of citizenship.

中文

歡迎您！移居到一個新國家需要勇氣，而申請成為加拿大公民又是另一個重大決定。您正加入由世代開拓者所建立的偉大傳統。一旦您符合所有法律要件，我們期盼以「享有公民全部權利與義務」的新公民身分歡迎您。

Discover Canada / 發現加拿大

English

Canada has welcomed generations of newcomers to our shores to help us build a free, law-abiding and prosperous society. For 400 years, settlers and immigrants have contributed to the diversity and richness of our country, which is built on a proud history and a strong identity.

中文

加拿大歡迎一代又一代的新移民來到這片土地，協助我們建立一個自由、守法、繁榮的社會。四百年來，移居者與移民為這個建立在驕傲歷史與堅強身分認同之上的國家貢獻了多元與豐富。

English

Canada is a constitutional monarchy, a parliamentary democracy and a federal state. Canadians are bound together by a shared commitment to the rule of law and to the institutions of parliamentary government.

中文

加拿大是 君主立憲制 (constitutional monarchy)、議會民主 (parliamentary democracy) 與 聯邦國家 (federal state)。加拿大人因共同信守 法治 (rule of law) 與議會政府制度而緊密相連。

English

Canadians take pride in their identity and have made sacrifices to defend their way of life. By coming to Canada and taking this important step toward Canadian citizenship, you are helping to write the continuing story of Canada.

中文

加拿大人以自己的身分為榮，並曾為了捍衛自己的生活方式做出犧牲。您來到加拿大、邁向公民身分的重要一步，也正在為加拿大的故事續寫篇章。

English

Immigrants between the ages of 18 and 54 must have adequate knowledge of English or French in order to become Canadian citizens. You must also learn about voting procedures, Canada’s history, symbols, democratic institutions, geography, and the rights and responsibilities of citizenship.

中文

18 至 54 歲的移民必須具備足夠的英文或法文能力才能成為公民。同時也必須認識 投票程序、加拿大歷史、國家象徵、民主制度、地理，以及公民的權利與義務。

English

Canadian citizens enjoy many rights, but Canadians also have responsibilities. They must obey Canada’s laws and respect the rights and freedoms of others.

中文

加拿大公民享有許多權利，但也承擔相應的責任。他們必須 遵守加拿大的法律 並 尊重他人的權利與自由。

English

This guide will help you prepare to become a Canadian citizen. Good luck!

中文

這份指南將幫助您準備成為加拿大公民。祝您順利！

Understanding the Oath / 認識誓詞

English

In Canada, we profess our loyalty to a person who represents all Canadians and not to a document such as a constitution, a banner such as a flag, or a geopolitical entity such as a country. In our constitutional monarchy, these elements are encompassed by the Sovereign (Queen or King). It is a remarkably simple yet powerful principle: Canada is personified by the Sovereign just as the Sovereign is personified by Canada.

中文

在加拿大，我們宣誓效忠的是一個 代表所有加拿大人的人物，而不是某份文件（如憲法）、某面旗幟、或某個地理政治實體（如國家）。在君主立憲制下，這些元素全部都由 君主 (the Sovereign，國王或女王) 所涵蓋。這是一個簡單卻有力的原則：加拿大由君主人格化，君主也由加拿大人格化。

The Oath of Citizenship / 入籍誓詞

🔴 2026 年現行版本（你考試時要背的版本）

English

I swear (or affirm) That I will be faithful And bear true allegiance To His Majesty King Charles the Third King of Canada His Heirs and Successors And that I will faithfully observe The laws of Canada Including the Constitution Which recognizes and affirms The Aboriginal and treaty rights of First Nations, Inuit and Métis peoples And fulfil my duties As a Canadian citizen.

中文

我謹宣誓（或鄭重聲明）： 我將忠誠並真心效忠 陛下查理三世 加拿大之 國王 及其繼承人與後嗣， 我將忠實遵守 加拿大的法律， 包括承認並確認 第一民族 (First Nations)、因紐特 (Inuit) 與梅蒂斯 (Métis) 等 原住民族與條約權利的憲法， 並善盡身為加拿大公民的職責。

📜 2021 PDF 原版（含 Queen Elizabeth，僅供對照認讀，不要背這個版本）

English

I swear (or affirm) / That I will be faithful / And bear true allegiance / To Her Majesty Queen Elizabeth the Second / Queen of Canada / Her Heirs and Successors / And that I will faithfully observe / The laws of Canada / Including the Constitution / Which recognizes and affirms / The Aboriginal and treaty rights of / First Nations, Inuit and Métis peoples / And fulfil my duties / As a Canadian citizen.

中文

（內容除了 King/Queen 名字之外與上方相同。）

背誦關鍵字

關鍵字 中文 為什麼會考
King Charles the Third 查理三世 2022 年起換新君，是 2026 年考試最常考改動
King of Canada 加拿大之國王 強調君主以「加拿大國王」身分受誓
Heirs and Successors 繼承人與後嗣 君主世襲制
the Constitution 憲法 法律來自憲法，憲法承認原住民權利
Aboriginal and treaty rights 原住民與條約權利 2021 年版新增的關鍵段落
First Nations, Inuit and Métis 第一民族、因紐特、梅蒂斯 3 大原住民族
fulfil my duties 履行職責 公民有 6 大責任，見下一章

上考場前 一字不漏背完上方紅色版本——口試官可能直接要求你背。

第 1 章：申請入籍 / Applying for Citizenship

對應 PDF 第 6–7 頁。對應 day-01.md。

Applying for Citizenship / 申請入籍

English

When you apply for citizenship, officials will check your status, verify that you are not prohibited from applying, and ensure that you meet the requirements.

中文

當您申請入籍時，政府官員會 核對您的身分、確認您未被限制申請，並 確認您符合所有要件。

English

Your application may take several months. Please ensure that the Call Centre always has your correct address while your application is being processed.

中文

您的申請可能需要 數個月 才會處理完畢。請確保在審理期間 客服中心 (Call Centre) 始終擁有您正確的地址。

See page 54 for telephone numbers.

電話號碼請見指南第 54 頁。

How to Use This Booklet to Prepare for the Citizenship Test / 如何使用本書準備公民考試

English

This booklet will help you prepare for the citizenship test. You should:

  • Study this guide;
  • Ask a friend or family member to help you practise answering questions about Canada;
  • Call a local school or school board, a college, a community centre or a local organization that provides services to immigrants and ask for information on citizenship classes;
  • Take English or French language classes, which the Government of Canada offers free of charge.
中文

本書將協助您準備公民考試。建議您：

  • 研讀本指南；
  • 請朋友或家人協助您練習回答 關於加拿大的問題；
  • 聯繫當地學校 / 教育局 / 學院 / 社區中心 / 移民服務機構，詢問是否有公民考試輔導課程；
  • 參加加拿大政府提供的免費英文或法文課程（LINC / CLIC）。

About the Citizenship Test / 關於公民考試

English

The citizenship test is usually a written test, but it could be an interview. You will be tested on two basic requirements for citizenship: 1) knowledge of Canada and of the rights and responsibilities of citizenship, and 2) adequate knowledge of English or French. Adult applicants 55 years of age and over do not need to write the citizenship test.

中文

公民考試 通常為筆試，但也可能改為面試。考試會檢測 兩項基本要件：(1) 對加拿大及公民權利與義務的認識；(2) 足夠的英文或法文能力。55 歲以上的成年申請人不需要參加公民考試。

English

The Citizenship Regulations provide information on how your ability to meet the knowledge of Canada requirement is determined. Information about this requirement can be found on page 64 of the study guide.

中文

《公民法規 (Citizenship Regulations)》規定如何判定您是否符合「對加拿大的認識」要件，詳情請見本指南第 64 頁。

English

All the citizenship test questions are based on the subject areas noted in the Citizenship Regulations, and all required information is provided in this study guide.

中文

所有公民考試題目皆出自《公民法規》所列的主題範圍，且 所有必備資訊都包含在本指南中。

After the Test / 考試之後

English

If you pass the test and meet all the other requirements, you will receive a Notice to Appear to Take the Oath of Citizenship. This document tells you the date, time and place of your citizenship ceremony.

中文

若您通過考試並符合所有其他要件，您將收到 「出席宣誓通知 (Notice to Appear to Take the Oath of Citizenship)」。此文件會告知您 入籍典禮 (citizenship ceremony) 的日期、時間與地點。

English

At the ceremony, you will:

  • Take the Oath of Citizenship;
  • Sign the oath form; and
  • Receive your Canadian Citizenship Certificate.
中文

典禮上您將：

  • 宣讀公民誓詞；
  • 簽署誓詞文件；以及
  • 領取加拿大公民身分證書 (Canadian Citizenship Certificate)。
English

If you do not pass the test, you will receive a notification indicating the next steps.

中文

若您未通過考試，您將收到通知，告知後續步驟（通常是 改安排面試 或 延期重考）。

English

You are encouraged to bring your family and friends to celebrate this occasion.

中文

典禮歡迎您 攜伴家人與朋友 一同慶祝這一刻。

重點整理

項目 內容
考試形式 通常筆試，可能改面試
考試範圍 本指南所列章節（所有題目都出自書內）
免試年齡 55 歲以上 不需筆試
通過後 收 Notice to Appear → 宣誓典禮 → 領 公民證書
未過後 收通知，告知下一步（通常是面試或重考）

第 2 章：公民的權利與義務 / Rights and Responsibilities of Citizenship

對應 PDF 第 8–9 頁。對應 day-01.md。

開場：法律的來源

English

Canadian citizens have rights and responsibilities. These come to us from our history, are secured by Canadian law, and reflect our shared traditions, identity and values.

中文

加拿大公民擁有權利與義務。這些權利義務 源自我們的歷史、由 加拿大法律所保障，並反映我們共同的傳統、身分認同與價值觀。

English

Canadian law has several sources, including laws passed by Parliament and the provincial legislatures, English common law, the civil code of France and the unwritten constitution that we have inherited from Great Britain.

中文

加拿大法律有 多重來源，包括：

  • 國會 (Parliament) 與省議會所通過的法律
  • 英國普通法 (English Common Law)
  • 法國民法 (Civil Code of France)——主要適用於魁北克省私法事務
  • 承襲自英國的不成文憲法 (Unwritten Constitution)

800 年的有序自由傳統

English

Together, these secure for Canadians an 800-year old tradition of ordered liberty, which dates back to the signing of Magna Carta in 1215 in England (also known as the Great Charter of Freedoms), including:

  • Freedom of conscience and religion;
  • Freedom of thought, belief, opinion and expression, including freedom of speech and of the press;
  • Freedom of peaceful assembly; and
  • Freedom of association.
中文

這些法律共同為加拿大人保障了一個 800 年悠久的有序自由傳統，可追溯至 1215 年英國《大憲章 Magna Carta》（又稱 Great Charter of Freedoms） 的簽訂，包含 4 大基本自由：

  1. 良知與宗教自由 (Freedom of conscience and religion)
  2. 思想、信仰、意見與表達自由（含言論與新聞自由）(Freedom of thought, belief, opinion and expression, including freedom of speech and of the press)
  3. 和平集會自由 (Freedom of peaceful assembly)
  4. 結社自由 (Freedom of association)
English

Habeas corpus, the right to challenge unlawful detention by the state, comes from English common law.

中文

人身保護令 (Habeas Corpus)——亦即 挑戰國家非法拘留的權利——源自 英國普通法。

1982 年憲法與《權利與自由憲章》

English

The Constitution of Canada was amended in 1982 to entrench the Canadian Charter of Rights and Freedoms, which begins with the words, “Whereas Canada is founded upon principles that recognize the supremacy of God and the rule of law.” This phrase underlines the importance of religious traditions to Canadian society and the dignity and worth of the human person.

中文

1982 年 加拿大憲法修訂，將 《加拿大權利與自由憲章 Canadian Charter of Rights and Freedoms》 正式入憲。憲章開頭寫道：「鑑於加拿大乃建立於 承認上帝的至高與法治 的原則之上 (Whereas Canada is founded upon principles that recognize the supremacy of God and the rule of law)。」這句話彰顯了 宗教傳統對加拿大社會的重要性，以及 每個人的尊嚴與價值。

憲章中的關鍵權利

English

The Charter attempts to summarize fundamental freedoms while also setting out additional rights. The most important of these include:

中文

憲章除了總結基本自由之外，也明確列出其他重要權利，其中最重要的包括：

English

Mobility Rights — Canadians can live and work anywhere they choose in Canada, enter and leave the country freely, and apply for a passport.

中文

遷徙權 (Mobility Rights)——加拿大人 可自由選擇在加拿大境內任何地方居住與工作、自由進出加拿大，並 申請護照。

English

Aboriginal Peoples’ Rights — The rights guaranteed in the Charter will not adversely affect any treaty or other rights or freedoms of Aboriginal peoples.

中文

原住民權利 (Aboriginal Peoples’ Rights)——憲章所保障的權利 不會對原住民既有的條約或其他權利與自由造成不利影響。

English

Official Language Rights and Minority Language Educational Rights — French and English have equal status in Parliament and throughout the government.

中文

官方語言權與少數語言教育權 (Official Language Rights and Minority Language Educational Rights)——法文與英文 在國會與整個聯邦政府中 享有平等地位。

English

Multiculturalism — A fundamental characteristic of the Canadian heritage and identity. Canadians celebrate the gift of one another’s presence and work hard to respect pluralism and live in harmony.

中文

多元文化主義 (Multiculturalism)——加拿大傳統與身分認同的根本特質之一。加拿大人 珍視彼此的存在，並 努力尊重多元、和諧共處。

Defending Canada / 保衛加拿大

English

There is no compulsory military service in Canada. However, serving in the regular Canadian forces (navy, army and air force) is a noble way to contribute to Canada and an excellent career choice (www.forces.ca). You can serve in your local part-time navy, militia and air reserves and gain valuable experience, skills and contacts. Young people can learn discipline, responsibility and skills by getting involved in the cadets (www.cadets.ca).

中文

加拿大沒有義務兵役制度。然而，加入 加拿大正規部隊（海軍、陸軍、空軍） 是一種 崇高的服務方式，也是 絕佳的職業選擇（www.forces.ca）。您也可以加入 當地兼職海軍預備、民兵或空軍預備役，獲得實貴經驗、技能與人脈。年輕人可加入 學員團 (cadets)（www.cadets.ca），培養紀律、責任感與技能。

English

You may also serve in the Coast Guard or emergency services in your community such as a police force or fire department. By helping to protect your community, you follow in the footsteps of Canadians before you who made sacrifices in the service of our country.

中文

您也可以加入 加拿大海岸巡防隊 (Coast Guard)，或社區的 緊急服務（警察、消防）。透過保護社區，您正在 承襲前人為國家服務犧牲奉獻的腳步。

The Equality of Women and Men / 男女平等

English

In Canada, men and women are equal under the law. Canada’s openness and generosity do not extend to barbaric cultural practices that tolerate spousal abuse, “honour killings,” female genital mutilation, forced marriage or other gender-based violence. Those guilty of these crimes are severely punished under Canada’s criminal laws.

中文

在加拿大，男女在法律上完全平等。加拿大的開放與包容 並不延伸到野蠻文化習俗——例如 配偶虐待 (spousal abuse)、「榮譽謀殺」(honour killings)、女性生殖器切割 (female genital mutilation)、強迫婚姻 (forced marriage) 或其他 性別暴力 (gender-based violence)。觸犯這些罪行者，將依加拿大刑法 受到嚴厲制裁。

Citizenship Responsibilities / 公民的責任

English

In Canada, rights come with responsibilities. These include:

中文

在加拿大，權利伴隨責任 而來。公民責任包括：

English

Obeying the law — One of Canada’s founding principles is the rule of law. Individuals and governments are regulated by laws and not by arbitrary actions. No person or group is above the law.

中文

1. 遵守法律 (Obeying the law)——法治 (rule of law) 是加拿大的立國原則之一。個人與政府 都受法律規範，而非任意行事。沒有任何個人或團體可凌駕於法律之上。

English

Taking responsibility for oneself and one’s family — Getting a job, taking care of one’s family and working hard in keeping with one’s abilities are important Canadian values. Work contributes to personal dignity and self-respect, and to Canada’s prosperity.

中文

2. 為自己與家人負責 (Taking responsibility for oneself and one’s family)——就業、照顧家庭、依個人能力努力工作 是重要的加拿大價值觀。勞動有助於個人尊嚴、自尊，並促進加拿大的繁榮。

English

Serving on a jury — When called to do so, you are legally required to serve. Serving on a jury is a privilege that makes the justice system work as it depends on impartial juries made up of citizens.

中文

3. 擔任陪審員 (Serving on a jury)——一旦被傳喚，您依法必須出庭服務。擔任陪審員是一項 特權——加拿大的司法系統依賴 由公民組成的公正陪審團 才能運作。

English

Voting in elections — The right to vote comes with a responsibility to vote in federal, provincial or territorial and local elections.

中文

4. 參與投票 (Voting in elections)——投票權同時帶有 在聯邦、省/領地、地方各級選舉中投票的責任。

English

Helping others in the community — Millions of volunteers freely donate their time to help others without pay—helping people in need, assisting at your child’s school, volunteering at a food bank or other charity, or encouraging newcomers to integrate. Volunteering is an excellent way to gain useful skills and develop friends and contacts.

中文

5. 協助社區 (Helping others in the community)——數百萬名 志工 無償付出時間：幫助有需要的人、協助孩子的學校、在食物銀行或慈善機構服務、協助新移民融入。志工服務是培養技能與建立人脈的絕佳方式。

English

Protecting and enjoying our heritage and environment — Every citizen has a role to play in avoiding waste and pollution while protecting Canada’s natural, cultural and architectural heritage for future generations.

中文

6. 保護並享受我們的遺產與環境 (Protecting and enjoying our heritage and environment)——每位公民都有責任 避免浪費與污染，並 為下一代保護加拿大的自然、文化與建築遺產。

重點記憶卡

4 大基本自由（憲章）

  1. 良知與宗教
  2. 思想、信仰、意見、表達（含言論與新聞）
  3. 和平集會
  4. 結社

6 大公民責任（Discover Canada 直接列出，超常考！）

  1. 守法
  2. 照顧自己與家人
  3. 擔任陪審員
  4. 投票
  5. 協助社區
  6. 保護並享受加拿大的遺產與環境

法律來源 4 條

英國普通法 + 法國民法 + 國會/省議會立法 + 英國不成文憲法

關鍵英文片語

  • Rule of law（法治）
  • Magna Carta 1215（大憲章）
  • Habeas corpus（人身保護令）
  • Canadian Charter of Rights and Freedoms 1982（加拿大權利與自由憲章）
  • “Whereas Canada is founded upon principles that recognize the supremacy of God and the rule of law”（憲章開頭）

第 3 章：我們是誰 / Who We Are

對應 PDF 第 10–13 頁。對應 day-02.md。

Unity in Diversity / 多元中的統一

English

John Buchan, the 1st Baron Tweedsmuir, was a popular Governor General of Canada (1935–40). Immigrant groups, he said, “should retain their individuality and each make its contribution to the national character.” Each could learn “from the other, and … while they cherish their own special loyalties and traditions, they cherish not less that new loyalty and tradition which springs from their union.” (Canadian Club of Halifax, 1937).

中文

John Buchan（第一代特威茲穆爾男爵） 是 1935–40 年廣受愛戴的加拿大總督。他曾說：移民族群「應該保有自身的獨特性，並各自為國家性格做出貢獻」；各族群之間可以彼此學習，「他們在珍視自身忠誠與傳統的同時，也同樣珍視從相互結合中誕生的新忠誠與新傳統」（1937 年於哈利法克斯加拿大俱樂部演講）。

加拿大的自由與身分

English

Canada is known around the world as a strong and free country. Canadians are proud of their unique identity. We have inherited the oldest continuous constitutional tradition in the world. We are the only constitutional monarchy in North America.

中文

加拿大舉世聞名為一個 強盛而自由的國家。加拿大人以自身獨特的身分認同為榮。我們繼承了 全世界最古老且延續至今的憲政傳統，並且是 北美唯一的君主立憲國家。

English

Our institutions uphold a commitment to Peace, Order and Good Government, a key phrase in Canada’s original constitutional document in 1867, the British North America Act. A belief in ordered liberty, enterprise, hard work and fair play has enabled Canadians to build a prosperous society in a rugged environment from our Atlantic shores to the Pacific Ocean and to the Arctic Circle—so much so that poets and songwriters have hailed Canada as the “Great Dominion.”

中文

我們的制度信守 「和平、秩序與良政」(Peace, Order and Good Government)——這是 1867 年《英屬北美法案 (British North America Act)》 的關鍵詞。對 有序自由、企業精神、勤奮工作與公平競爭 的信念，使加拿大人在 從大西洋岸、太平洋岸到北極圈 的嚴苛環境中建立繁榮社會——以至於詩人與作詞者讚譽加拿大為 「偉大的自治領 (Great Dominion)」。

English

To understand what it means to be Canadian, it is important to know about our three founding peoples— Aboriginal, French and British.

中文

要理解「成為加拿大人」的意涵，必須認識我們的 三大開國民族 (three founding peoples)：原住民 (Aboriginal)、法裔 (French) 與英裔 (British)。

Aboriginal Peoples / 原住民

English

The ancestors of Aboriginal peoples are believed to have migrated from Asia many thousands of years ago. They were well established here long before explorers from Europe first came to North America. Diverse, vibrant First Nations cultures were rooted in religious beliefs about their relationship to the Creator, the natural environment and each other.

中文

原住民的祖先 被認為是數千年前從亞洲遷徙而來。早在歐洲探險家抵達北美之前，他們已在此安居樂業。多元而活躍的 第一民族文化 根植於 與造物主、自然環境與彼此關係的宗教信仰。

English

Aboriginal and treaty rights are in the Canadian Constitution. Territorial rights were first guaranteed through the Royal Proclamation of 1763 by King George III, and established the basis for negotiating treaties with the newcomers—treaties that were not always fully respected.

中文

原住民權利與條約權利已寫入加拿大憲法。領土權 最早由 英王喬治三世於 1763 年的《皇家公告 (Royal Proclamation of 1763)》 所保障，並奠定了與新移居者談判條約的基礎——這些條約並非總是被完全遵守。

English

From the 1800s until the 1980s, the federal government placed many Aboriginal children in residential schools to educate and assimilate them into mainstream Canadian culture. The schools were poorly funded and inflicted hardship on the students; some were physically abused. Aboriginal languages and cultural practices were mostly prohibited. In 2008, Ottawa formally apologized to the former students.

中文

從 1800 年代直到 1980 年代，聯邦政府將許多原住民兒童送入 寄宿學校 (residential schools)，企圖將他們同化至主流加拿大文化。這些學校 資源匱乏、給學生帶來痛苦，部分學童更遭受 體罰。原住民語言與文化習俗大多被禁止。2008 年，渥太華（聯邦政府）正式向前學童道歉。

English

In today’s Canada, Aboriginal peoples enjoy renewed pride and confidence, and have made significant achievements in agriculture, the environment, business and the arts.

中文

在今日加拿大，原住民族重拾了驕傲與自信，在 農業、環境、商業與藝術 領域有重大成就。

原住民 3 大族群

English

Today, the term Aboriginal peoples refers to three distinct groups:

中文

「原住民 (Aboriginal peoples)」一詞涵蓋 3 個獨特的群體：

English

Indian refers to all Aboriginal people who are not Inuit or Métis. In the 1970s, the term First Nations began to be used. Today, about half of First Nations people live on reserve land in about 600 communities while the other half live off-reserve, mainly in urban centres.

中文

「印第安人 (Indian)」 指所有非因紐特、非梅蒂斯的原住民。1970 年代起改稱「第一民族 (First Nations)」。今日約 一半的第一民族居於約 600 個保留地 (reserves) 社區，另一半 居於保留地外的城市。

English

The Inuit, which means “the people” in the Inuktitut language, live in small, scattered communities across the Arctic. Their knowledge of the land, sea and wildlife enabled them to adapt to one of the harshest environments on earth.

中文

「因紐特 (Inuit)」 在 Inuktitut 語 中意為「人民」，他們居住在 北極區散布的小型社區。對土地、海洋與野生動物的知識 讓他們適應了地球上最嚴苛的環境之一。

English

The Métis are a distinct people of mixed Aboriginal and European ancestry, the majority of whom live in the Prairie provinces. They come from both French- and English-speaking backgrounds and speak their own dialect, Michif.

中文

「梅蒂斯 (Métis)」 是 原住民與歐裔混血 的獨特族群，大多居於 草原省 (Prairie provinces)。他們同時有 法語與英語背景，講自己的方言 Michif。

English

About 65% of the Aboriginal people are First Nations, while 30% are Métis and 4% Inuit.

中文

原住民中約 65% 為第一民族 (First Nations)、30% 為梅蒂斯 (Métis)、4% 為因紐特 (Inuit)。

English and French / 英裔與法裔

English

Canadian society today stems largely from the English-speaking and French-speaking Christian civilizations that were brought here from Europe by settlers. English and French define the reality of day-to-day life for most people and are the country’s official languages. The federal government is required by law to provide services throughout Canada in English and French.

中文

今日的加拿大社會主要源自 歐洲移民帶來的英語與法語基督教文明。英文與法文 定義了多數人的日常生活，並是國家的 官方語言。聯邦政府依法必須在全國以英語與法語提供服務。

English

Today, there are 18 million Anglophones—people who speak English as a first language—and seven million Francophones—people who speak French as their first language. While the majority of Francophones live in the province of Quebec, one million Francophones live in Ontario, New Brunswick and Manitoba, with a smaller presence in other provinces. New Brunswick is the only officially bilingual province.

中文

今日加拿大有 1,800 萬以英文為母語者 (Anglophones)，以及 700 萬以法文為母語者 (Francophones)。大部分法裔居住於 魁北克省，但約有 100 萬法裔居於安大略、新布朗斯維克與曼尼托巴，其他省份亦有少數。新布朗斯維克 (New Brunswick) 是加拿大唯一官方雙語省。

English

The Acadians are the descendants of French colonists who began settling in what are now the Maritime provinces in 1604. Between 1755 and 1763, during the war between Britain and France, more than two-thirds of the Acadians were deported from their homeland. Despite this ordeal, known as the “Great Upheaval,” the Acadians survived and maintained their unique identity. Today, Acadian culture is flourishing and is a lively part of French-speaking Canada.

中文

「阿卡迪亞人 (Acadians)」 是 1604 年起定居於今日海岸省份 (Maritime provinces) 的法國殖民者後裔。1755–1763 英法戰爭期間，三分之二以上的阿卡迪亞人被驅逐出家園——史稱 「大動盪 (Great Upheaval)」。儘管如此，他們 倖存並保有獨特身分。今日 阿卡迪亞文化蓬勃發展，是法語加拿大充滿活力的一部分。

English

Quebecers are the people of Quebec, the vast majority French-speaking. Most are descendants of 8,500 French settlers from the 1600s and 1700s and maintain a unique identity, culture and language. The House of Commons recognized in 2006 that the Quebecois form a nation within a united Canada. One million Anglo-Quebecers have a heritage of 250 years and form a vibrant part of the Quebec fabric.

中文

「魁北克人 (Quebecers)」 是魁北克的居民，絕大多數講法語。多數為 1600 與 1700 年代 8,500 名法國移居者的後裔，保有獨特的身分、文化與語言。2006 年，加拿大眾議院承認魁北克人 (Quebecois) 為「統一加拿大內的一個民族 (a nation within a united Canada)」。100 萬名英裔魁北克人 (Anglo-Quebecers) 擁有 250 年的歷史傳承，是魁北克社會充滿活力的一部分。

English Settlers / 英裔移居者

English

The basic way of life in English-speaking areas was established by hundreds of thousands of English, Welsh, Scottish and Irish settlers, soldiers and migrants from the 1600s to the 20th century. Generations of pioneers and builders of British origins, as well as other groups, invested and endured hardship in laying the foundations of our country. This helps explain why Anglophones (English speakers) are generally referred to as English Canadians.

中文

英語區的基本生活方式由 1600 年代至 20 世紀數十萬名英格蘭、威爾斯、蘇格蘭與愛爾蘭 的移居者、士兵與移民所建立。一代代英國裔的開拓者與建設者（以及其他族群）投入心血並承擔艱辛，奠定了我們國家的根基。這也是 英語使用者一般被稱為「英裔加拿大人 (English Canadians)」 的原因。

Becoming Canadian / 成為加拿大人

English

Some Canadians immigrate from places where they have experienced warfare or conflict. Such experiences do not justify bringing to Canada violent, extreme or hateful prejudices. In becoming Canadian, newcomers are expected to embrace democratic principles such as the rule of law.

中文

部分加拿大人來自曾經歷戰爭或衝突之地。這些經歷不能成為將暴力、極端或仇恨偏見帶入加拿大的藉口。成為加拿大人意味著接納民主原則，例如法治。

Diversity in Canada / 加拿大的多元

English

The majority of Canadians were born in this country and this has been true since the 1800s. However, Canada is often referred to as a land of immigrants because, over the past 200 years, millions of newcomers have helped to build and defend our way of life.

中文

自 1800 年代起，多數加拿大人即出生於本土。然而加拿大常被稱為 「移民之國 (a land of immigrants)」，因為過去 200 年裡，數百萬新移民協助建設並捍衛了我們的生活方式。

English

Many ethnic and religious groups live and work in peace as proud Canadians. The largest groups are the English, French, Scottish, Irish, German, Italian, Chinese, Aboriginal, Ukrainian, Dutch, South Asian and Scandinavian. Since the 1970s, most immigrants have come from Asian countries.

中文

許多族裔與宗教群體 以驕傲的加拿大人身分和平共處。最大的群體包括 英格蘭、法蘭西、蘇格蘭、愛爾蘭、德國、義大利、華人、原住民、烏克蘭、荷蘭、南亞、北歐。1970 年代起，多數新移民來自亞洲國家。

English

Non-official languages are widely spoken in Canadian homes. Chinese languages are the second most-spoken at home, after English, in two of Canada’s biggest cities. In Vancouver, 13% of the population speak Chinese languages at home; in Toronto, the number is 7%.

中文

非官方語言 在加拿大家庭中廣泛使用。在兩個最大城市中，中文是僅次於英文的第二大家用語言：溫哥華 13%、多倫多 7% 在家講中文。

English

The great majority of Canadians identify as Christians. The largest religious affiliation is Catholic, followed by various Protestant churches. The numbers of Muslims, Jews, Hindus, Sikhs and members of other religions, as well as people who state “no religion” are also growing.

中文

絕大多數加拿大人 自認為基督徒。最大的宗教派別是 天主教 (Catholic)，其次是 各新教教派 (Protestant)。穆斯林、猶太教徒、印度教徒、錫克教徒 與其他宗教信徒、以及 「無宗教信仰者」 的人數也持續增加。

English

In Canada the state has traditionally partnered with faith communities to promote social welfare, harmony and mutual respect; to provide schools and health care; to resettle refugees; and to uphold religious freedom, religious expression and freedom of conscience.

中文

在加拿大，國家傳統上與宗教社群合作，以促進 社會福利、和諧與相互尊重；提供 學校與健保；安置難民；並維護 宗教自由、宗教表達與良知自由。

English

Canada’s diversity includes gay and lesbian Canadians, who enjoy the full protection of and equal treatment under the law, including access to civil marriage.

中文

加拿大的多元也涵蓋 同性戀加拿大人 (gay and lesbian Canadians)，他們在法律之下 受完全保障與平等對待，包括 可進入民事婚姻 (civil marriage)。

English

Together, these diverse groups, sharing a common Canadian identity, make up today’s multicultural society.

中文

這些多元族群共同擁有加拿大人身分，組成了今日加拿大的多元文化社會。

English

Olympian Marjorie Turner-Bailey of Nova Scotia is a descendant of black Loyalists, escaped slaves and freed men and women of African origin who in the 1780s fled to Canada from America, where slavery remained legal until 1863.

中文

新斯科細亞的奧運選手 Marjorie Turner-Bailey 是 黑人保皇黨 (black Loyalists)、逃亡黑奴與獲釋黑人 的後裔——他們在 1780 年代 從仍實施奴隸制度的美國（奴隸制度在美國延續到 1863 年）逃到加拿大。

第 4 章：加拿大歷史 / Canada’s History

English

對應 PDF 第 14–23 頁。對應 day-03.md、day-04.md、day-05.md。

中文

（未提供翻譯）

Aboriginal Peoples / 原住民

English

When Europeans explored Canada they found all regions occupied by native peoples they called Indians, because the first explorers thought they had reached the East Indies. The native people lived off the land, some by hunting and gathering, others by raising crops. The Huron-Wendat of the Great Lakes region, like the Iroquois, were farmers and hunters. The Cree and Dene of the Northwest were hunter-gatherers. The Sioux were nomadic, following the bison (buffalo) herd. The Inuit lived off Arctic wildlife. West Coast natives preserved fish by drying and smoking. Warfare was common among Aboriginal groups as they competed for land, resources and prestige.

中文

當歐洲人探勘加拿大時，發現所有地區都已有原住民居住，最初被誤稱為 「印第安人 (Indians)」（因為最早的探險家以為他們抵達了東印度群島）。原住民依土地維生——有人 狩獵採集，有人 耕作：

  • 五大湖區的 Huron-Wendat 與 易洛魁 Iroquois：農耕並狩獵
  • 西北地區的 Cree 與 Dene：狩獵採集
  • Sioux：游牧，逐 野牛 (bison) 而居
  • Inuit：以北極野生動物為生
  • 西海岸原住民：以 乾燥與燻製 保存魚類

各族群之間 常為了土地、資源與聲望而戰爭。

English

The arrival of European traders, missionaries, soldiers and colonists changed the native way of life forever. Large numbers of Aboriginals died of European diseases to which they lacked immunity. However, Aboriginals and Europeans formed strong economic, religious and military bonds in the first 200 years of coexistence which laid the foundations of Canada.

中文

歐洲商人、傳教士、士兵與殖民者的抵達 永遠改變了原住民的生活方式。大量原住民死於缺乏免疫力的歐洲疾病。然而在共存的前 200 年裡，原住民與歐洲人在經濟、宗教與軍事上建立了強而有力的連結，奠定了加拿大的基礎。

The First Europeans / 最早的歐洲人

English

The Vikings from Iceland who colonized Greenland 1,000 years ago also reached Labrador and the island of Newfoundland. The remains of their settlement, l’Anse aux Meadows, are a World Heritage site.

中文

1,000 年前 來自冰島並殖民格陵蘭的 維京人 (Vikings) 也到達了 拉布拉多與紐芬蘭島。他們的聚落遺跡 L’Anse aux Meadows 現為 世界文化遺產。

English

European exploration began in earnest in 1497 with the expedition of John Cabot, who was the first to draw a map of Canada’s East Coast.

中文

歐洲探索 正式始於 1497 年 義裔英國探險家 John Cabot 的航行——他是 第一個繪製加拿大東岸地圖的人。

Exploring a River, Naming Canada / 探勘聖羅倫斯河、命名加拿大

English

Between 1534 and 1542, Jacques Cartier made three voyages across the Atlantic, claiming the land for King Francis I of France. Cartier heard two captured guides speak the Iroquoian word kanata, meaning “village.” By the 1550s, the name of Canada began appearing on maps.

中文

1534 至 1542 年間，Jacques Cartier 三度橫越大西洋，為法王法蘭索瓦一世 (Francis I) 宣稱土地。Cartier 從兩位被俘的嚮導口中聽到易洛魁語的 「kanata」，意為 「村莊」。1550 年代起，「Canada」之名開始出現在地圖上。

Royal New France / 新法蘭西

English

In 1604, the first European settlement north of Florida was established by French explorers Pierre de Monts and Samuel de Champlain, first on St. Croix Island (in present-day Maine), then at Port-Royal, in Acadia (present-day Nova Scotia). In 1608 Champlain built a fortress at what is now Québec City.

中文

1604 年，法國探險家 Pierre de Monts 與 Samuel de Champlain 建立了 佛羅里達以北最早的歐洲聚落——先在 St. Croix Island（今美國緬因州），後遷至 Port-Royal（阿卡迪亞，即今新斯科細亞）。1608 年，Champlain 在今魁北克市興建堡壘。

English

The colonists struggled against a harsh climate. Champlain allied the colony with the Algonquin, Montagnais and Huron, historic enemies of the Iroquois, a confederation of five (later six) First Nations who battled with the French settlements for a century. The French and the Iroquois made peace in 1701.

中文

殖民者面對嚴酷氣候奮鬥求生。Champlain 與 Algonquin、Montagnais、Huron 結盟——這些族群是 易洛魁聯盟 (Iroquois) 的歷史宿敵。易洛魁是由 5（後為 6）個第一民族組成的聯盟，與法國殖民地交戰了一個世紀。法、易雙方於 1701 年締結和平。

English

The French and Aboriginal people collaborated in the vast fur-trade economy, driven by the demand for beaver pelts in Europe. Outstanding leaders like Jean Talon, Bishop Laval, and Count Frontenac built a French Empire in North America that reached from Hudson Bay to the Gulf of Mexico.

中文

法國人與原住民合作經營龐大的 毛皮貿易經濟——以歐洲對 河狸皮 (beaver pelts) 的需求為驅動。Jean Talon、Bishop Laval、Count Frontenac 等傑出領袖在北美建立了一個 從哈德遜灣延伸到墨西哥灣 的法國帝國。

Struggle for a Continent / 爭奪大陸

English

In 1670, King Charles II of England granted the Hudson’s Bay Company exclusive trading rights over the watershed draining into Hudson Bay. For the next 100 years the Company competed with Montreal-based traders. The skilled and courageous men who travelled by canoe were called voyageurs and coureurs des bois, and formed strong alliances with First Nations.

中文

1670 年，英王查理二世 (Charles II) 授予 哈德遜灣公司 (Hudson’s Bay Company) 對 哈德遜灣集水區的獨家貿易權。此後 100 年間，該公司與蒙特婁的商人競爭。乘獨木舟旅行的勇敢人們被稱為 voyageurs 與 coureurs des bois，他們與第一民族建立了強大同盟。

English

English colonies along the Atlantic seaboard, dating from the early 1600s, eventually became richer and more populous than New France. In the 1700s France and Great Britain battled for control of North America. In 1759, the British defeated the French in the Battle of the Plains of Abraham at Québec City — marking the end of France’s empire in America. The commanders of both armies, Brigadier James Wolfe and the Marquis de Montcalm, were killed leading their troops in battle.

中文

1600 年代初 沿大西洋海岸的英國殖民地，最終變得 比新法蘭西更富裕、更人多。1700 年代 法國與英國爭奪北美的控制權。1759 年，英軍在魁北克市的「亞伯拉罕平原戰役 (Battle of the Plains of Abraham)」擊敗法軍——法國在美洲的帝國從此結束。雙方主將 英軍 Brigadier James Wolfe 與 法軍 Marquis de Montcalm 皆於戰鬥中陣亡。

The Province of Quebec / 魁北克省

English

Following the war, Great Britain renamed the colony the “Province of Quebec.” The French-speaking Catholic people, known as habitants or Canadiens, strove to preserve their way of life in the English-speaking, Protestant-ruled British Empire.

中文

戰後英國將殖民地改名為 「魁北克省 (Province of Quebec)」。法語天主教居民——稱為 habitants 或 Canadiens——在 英語、新教為主的大英帝國 中努力保存自己的生活方式。

A Tradition of Accommodation / 包容的傳統

English

To better govern the French Roman Catholic majority, the British Parliament passed the Quebec Act of 1774. One of the constitutional foundations of Canada, the Quebec Act accommodated the principles of British institutions to the reality of the province. It allowed religious freedom for Catholics and permitted them to hold public office, a practice not then allowed in Britain. The Quebec Act restored French civil law while maintaining British criminal law.

中文

為了治理人數佔多數的法裔天主教徒，英國國會於 1774 年通過《魁北克法案 (Quebec Act)》。這是 加拿大憲政基礎之一——它使英國制度的原則 配合該省的現實：

  • 允許天主教徒享有宗教自由
  • 允許天主教徒擔任公職（在當時英國本土尚不允許）
  • 恢復法國民法 (civil law)，同時 保留英國刑法 (criminal law)

United Empire Loyalists / 保皇黨

English

In 1776, the 13 British colonies to the south of Quebec declared independence and formed the United States. North America was again divided by war. More than 40,000 people loyal to the Crown, called “Loyalists,” fled the oppression of the American Revolution to settle in Nova Scotia and Quebec. Joseph Brant led thousands of Loyalist Mohawk Indians into Canada. The Loyalists came from Dutch, German, British, Scandinavian, Aboriginal and other origins and from Presbyterian, Anglican, Baptist, Methodist, Jewish, Quaker and Catholic religious backgrounds.

中文

1776 年，魁北克以南的 13 個英屬殖民地宣布獨立，組成美國。北美再度因戰爭分裂。超過 40,000 名效忠王室的「保皇黨 (Loyalists)」 逃避美國革命的壓迫，定居於 新斯科細亞與魁北克。Joseph Brant 領數千名 Mohawk 印第安保皇黨進入加拿大。Loyalists 包含 荷蘭、德國、英國、北歐、原住民等族裔，宗教上來自 長老會、聖公會、浸信會、衛理公會、猶太教、貴格會、天主教 等背景。

English

About 3,000 black Loyalists, freedmen and slaves came north seeking a better life. In turn, in 1792, some black Nova Scotians, who were given poor land, moved on to establish Freetown, Sierra Leone (West Africa), a new British colony for freed slaves.

中文

約 3,000 名黑人保皇黨、獲釋黑人與奴隸 北上尋求更好的生活。1792 年，部分被分配到貧瘠土地的黑人新斯科細亞人 移居西非，建立 獅子山 (Sierra Leone) 的自由城 (Freetown)——一個給獲釋黑奴的新英屬殖民地。

The Beginnings of Democracy / 民主的萌芽

English

Democratic institutions developed gradually and peacefully. The first representative assembly was elected in Halifax, Nova Scotia, in 1758. Prince Edward Island followed in 1773, New Brunswick in 1785. The Constitutional Act of 1791 divided the Province of Quebec into Upper Canada (later Ontario), which was mainly Loyalist, Protestant and English-speaking, and Lower Canada (later Quebec), heavily Catholic and French-speaking.

中文

民主制度逐步且和平地發展。首個代議議會於 1758 年在新斯科細亞哈利法克斯選出。愛德華王子島於 1773、新布朗斯維克於 1785 跟進。1791 年《憲法法案 (Constitutional Act)》 將魁北克省分為：

  • 上加拿大 (Upper Canada，即今 Ontario)：以保皇黨、新教、英語為主
  • 下加拿大 (Lower Canada，即今 Quebec)：以天主教與法語為主
English

The Act also granted to the Canadas, for the first time, legislative assemblies elected by the people. The name Canada also became official at this time and has been used ever since. The Atlantic colonies and the two Canadas were known collectively as British North America.

中文

該法案 首次賦予兩個加拿大省由人民選舉的立法議會。「Canada」之名自此正式使用。大西洋殖民地與兩個加拿大省合稱 「英屬北美 (British North America)」。

Abolition of Slavery / 廢除奴隸制

English

Slavery has existed all over the world, from Asia, Africa and the Middle East to the Americas. The first movement to abolish the transatlantic slave trade emerged in the British Parliament in the late 1700s. In 1793, Upper Canada, led by Lieutenant Governor John Graves Simcoe, a Loyalist military officer, became the first province in the Empire to move toward abolition. In 1807, the British Parliament prohibited the buying and selling of slaves, and in 1833 abolished slavery throughout the Empire. Thousands of slaves escaped from the United States, followed “the North Star” and settled in Canada via the Underground Railroad, a Christian anti-slavery network.

中文

奴隸制度全球皆有。廢除跨大西洋奴隸貿易的運動於 1700 年代末 從 英國國會 開始。1793 年，上加拿大在副省督 John Graves Simcoe（保皇黨軍官）的領導下，成為大英帝國第一個朝廢奴邁進的省份。1807 年英國國會禁止奴隸買賣，1833 年全帝國廢除奴隸制度。數千名美國奴隸 沿「北極星」，透過 「地下鐵路 (Underground Railroad)」——一個基督教反奴隸網絡——逃到加拿大定居。

A Growing Economy / 經濟成長

English

The first companies in Canada were formed during the French and British regimes and competed for the fur trade. The Hudson’s Bay Company, with French, British and Aboriginal employees, came to dominate the trade in the northwest from Fort Garry (Winnipeg) and Fort Edmonton to Fort Langley (near Vancouver) and Fort Victoria—trading posts that later became cities.

中文

加拿大最早的公司在法國與英國時期形成，競爭毛皮貿易。哈德遜灣公司 雇用法、英與原住民員工，從 Fort Garry（Winnipeg）、Fort Edmonton 到 Fort Langley（Vancouver 附近）與 Fort Victoria ——這些貿易據點後來都成為城市。

English

The first financial institutions opened in the late 18th and early 19th centuries. The Montreal Stock Exchange opened in 1832. For centuries Canada’s economy was based mainly on farming and on exporting natural resources such as fur, fish and timber, transported by roads, lakes, rivers and canals.

中文

最早的金融機構出現在 18 世紀末與 19 世紀初。蒙特婁證券交易所於 1832 年開業。幾個世紀以來，加拿大經濟以 農業與毛皮、漁、木材等自然資源出口 為主，透過 公路、湖泊、河流與運河 運輸。

The War of 1812: The Fight for Canada / 1812 年戰爭

English

After the defeat of Napoleon Bonaparte’s fleet in the Battle of Trafalgar (1805), the Royal Navy ruled the waves. The British Empire, which included Canada, fought to resist Bonaparte’s bid to dominate Europe. This led to American resentment at British interference with their shipping. Believing it would be easy to conquer Canada, the United States launched an invasion in June 1812.

中文

拿破崙艦隊於 1805 年的特拉法加海戰 (Battle of Trafalgar) 戰敗後，英國皇家海軍稱霸海洋。大英帝國（包括加拿大）對抗拿破崙的歐洲霸權。英國干涉美國船運引發美國不滿。美國以為可以輕易征服加拿大，於 1812 年 6 月發動入侵。

English

The Americans were mistaken. Canadian volunteers and First Nations, including Shawnee led by Chief Tecumseh, supported British soldiers in Canada’s defence. In July, Major-General Sir Isaac Brock captured Detroit but was killed while defending against an American attack at Queenston Heights, near Niagara Falls, a battle the Americans lost. In 1813, Lieutenant-Colonel Charles de Salaberry and 460 soldiers, mostly French Canadiens, turned back 4,000 American invaders at Châteauguay, south of Montreal.

中文

但美國錯了。加拿大志願軍與第一民族（包括 首領 Tecumseh 率領的 Shawnee）支援英軍防衛加拿大。7 月，少將 Sir Isaac Brock 攻下底特律，但在 尼加拉瀑布附近的 Queenston Heights 防衛美軍進攻時 陣亡——此戰美軍敗北。1813 年，中校 Charles de Salaberry 率 460 名士兵（多為法裔加拿大人），在蒙特婁南方的 Châteauguay 擊退 4,000 名美軍入侵者。

English

In 1813 the Americans burned Government House and the Parliament Buildings in York (now Toronto). In retaliation in 1814, Major-General Robert Ross led an expedition from Nova Scotia that burned down the White House and other public buildings in Washington, D.C. Ross died in battle soon afterwards and was buried in Halifax with full military honours.

中文

1813 年，美軍焚毀 York（今 Toronto）的政府辦公廳與國會建築。作為報復，1814 年少將 Robert Ross 率部隊從新斯科細亞出發，焚毀華盛頓特區的白宮與其他公共建築。Ross 不久後戰死，於哈利法克斯以全套軍禮安葬。

English

By 1814, the American attempt to conquer Canada had failed. The British paid for a costly Canadian defence system, including the Citadels at Halifax and Québec City, the naval drydock at Halifax and Fort Henry at Kingston—today popular historic sites. The present-day Canada-U.S.A. border is partly an outcome of the War of 1812, which ensured that Canada would remain independent of the United States.

中文

1814 年美國征服加拿大的企圖宣告失敗。今天的加美邊界部分即是 1812 戰爭的結果——這場戰爭確保了加拿大不被美國併吞。

English

In 1813, Laura Secord, pioneer wife and mother of five children, made a dangerous 19-mile (30-km) journey on foot to warn Lieutenant James FitzGibbon of a planned American attack. Her bravery contributed to victory at the Battle of Beaver Dams. She is recognized as a heroine to this day.

中文

1813 年，五個孩子的母親、開拓者妻子 Laura Secord，徒步走了 19 英里（30 公里）的危險旅程，警告 FitzGibbon 中尉美軍即將進攻。她的勇氣促成了 Beaver Dams 戰役 的勝利，她至今仍被尊為英雄。

Rebellions of 1837–38 / 1837–38 叛亂

English

In the 1830s, reformers in Upper and Lower Canada believed that progress toward full democracy was too slow. Some believed Canada should adopt American republican values or even try to join the United States. When armed rebellions occurred in 1837–38 in the area outside Montreal and in Toronto, the rebels did not have enough public support to succeed. They were defeated by British troops and Canadian volunteers. A number of rebels were hanged or exiled; some exiles later returned to Canada.

中文

1830 年代，上下加拿大的改革派認為民主進展太慢。部分人主張加拿大應採美式共和價值，甚至加入美國。1837–38 年蒙特婁外圍與多倫多爆發武裝叛亂，但叛軍 未獲足夠民意支持，被英軍與加拿大志願軍擊敗。部分叛亂者被處絞刑或流放，部分流放者後回到加拿大。

English

Lord Durham, an English reformer sent to report on the rebellions, recommended that Upper and Lower Canada be merged and given responsible government. This meant that the ministers of the Crown must have the support of a majority of the elected representatives in order to govern. Controversially, Lord Durham also said that the quickest way for the Canadiens to achieve progress was to assimilate into English-speaking Protestant culture. This recommendation demonstrated a complete lack of understanding of French Canadians, who sought to uphold the distinct identity of French Canada.

中文

英國改革派 Lord Durham 受派調查叛亂，建議 將上下加拿大合併並給予責任政府 (responsible government)——意即 內閣大臣必須獲得多數民選代表支持才能執政。但 Durham 也具爭議地主張 法裔加拿大人最快的進步方式就是同化於英語新教文化——這顯示他 完全不了解法裔加拿大人 想要維護自身獨特身分的願望。

English

Some reformers, including Sir Étienne-Paschal Taché and Sir George-Étienne Cartier, later became Fathers of Confederation, as did a former member of the voluntary government militia in Upper Canada, Sir John A. Macdonald.

中文

部分改革派，包括 Sir Étienne-Paschal Taché 和 Sir George-Étienne Cartier，後來成為 邦聯之父，John A. Macdonald 也是。

Responsible Government / 責任政府

English

In 1840, Upper and Lower Canada were united as the Province of Canada. Reformers such as Sir Louis-Hippolyte La Fontaine and Robert Baldwin, in parallel with Joseph Howe in Nova Scotia, worked with British governors toward responsible government.

中文

1840 年，上下加拿大合併為「加拿大省 (Province of Canada)」。改革派 Sir Louis-Hippolyte La Fontaine 與 Robert Baldwin，加上新斯科細亞的 Joseph Howe，與英國總督合作，推動 責任政府。

English

The first British North American colony to attain full responsible government was Nova Scotia in 1847–48. In 1848–49 the governor of United Canada, Lord Elgin, with encouragement from London, introduced responsible government.

中文

英屬北美最早達成完全責任政府的是 1847–48 年的新斯科細亞。1848–49 年，聯合加拿大總督 Lord Elgin 在倫敦的鼓勵下引入責任政府。

English

This is the system that we have today: if the government loses a confidence vote in the assembly it must resign. La Fontaine, a champion of democracy and French language rights, became the first leader of a responsible government in the Canadas.

中文

這就是我們今天的制度：若政府在議會中失去信任投票，必須下台。La Fontaine 成為加拿大首位責任政府領袖，是民主與法語權利的捍衛者。

Confederation / 邦聯

English

From 1864 to 1867, representatives of Nova Scotia, New Brunswick and the Province of Canada, with British support, worked together to establish a new country. These men are known as the Fathers of Confederation. They created two levels of government: federal and provincial. The old Province of Canada was split into two new provinces: Ontario and Quebec, which, together with New Brunswick and Nova Scotia, formed the new country called the Dominion of Canada. Each province would elect its own legislature and have control of such areas as education and health.

中文

1864–1867 年間，新斯科細亞、新布朗斯維克與加拿大省的代表在英國支持下合作建立新國家。這些人被稱為 「邦聯之父 (Fathers of Confederation)」。他們創建了 聯邦與省兩級政府。加拿大省被拆為 Ontario 和 Quebec，加上 New Brunswick 與 Nova Scotia，組成新國家 「加拿大自治領 (Dominion of Canada)」。每個省選舉自己的議會，負責 教育、健保 等領域。

English

The British Parliament passed the British North America Act in 1867. The Dominion of Canada was officially born on July 1, 1867. Until 1982, July 1 was celebrated as “Dominion Day” to commemorate the day that Canada became a self-governing Dominion. Today it is officially known as Canada Day.

中文

英國國會於 1867 年通過《英屬北美法案 (British North America Act)》。加拿大自治領於 1867 年 7 月 1 日正式誕生。直到 1982 年，7 月 1 日被稱為 「自治領日 (Dominion Day)」；今日正式稱為 「加拿大日 (Canada Day)」。

Dominion from Sea to Sea / 從海到海的自治領

English

Sir Leonard Tilley, an elected official and Father of Confederation from New Brunswick, suggested the term Dominion of Canada in 1864. He was inspired by Psalm 72 in the Bible which refers to “dominion from sea to sea and from the river to the ends of the earth.” This phrase embodied the vision of building a powerful, united, wealthy and free country that spanned a continent.

中文

來自新布朗斯維克的民選官員、邦聯之父 Sir Leonard Tilley，於 1864 年提議 「加拿大自治領 (Dominion of Canada)」 這個名稱。他的靈感來自聖經 詩篇 72 篇：「dominion from sea to sea and from the river to the ends of the earth（從海到海，從大河延及地極的權柄）」。這句話體現了 建立一個橫跨大陸、強大、團結、富裕、自由國家 的願景。

Canada’s First Prime Minister / 加拿大首任總理

English

In 1867, Sir John Alexander Macdonald, a Father of Confederation, became Canada’s first Prime Minister. Born in Scotland on January 11, 1815, he came to Upper Canada as a child. He was a lawyer in Kingston, Ontario, a gifted politician and a colourful personality. Parliament has recognized January 11 as Sir John A. Macdonald Day. His portrait is on the $10 bill.

中文

1867 年，邦聯之父 Sir John Alexander Macdonald 成為加拿大首任總理。他 1815 年 1 月 11 日生於蘇格蘭，幼年隨家人移居上加拿大，後在 Ontario 京斯敦執律師業，是一位有才華且個性鮮明的政治家。國會將 1 月 11 日定為 Sir John A. Macdonald Day。他的肖像曾印在 $10 紙鈔上（注：2018 年後 $10 已改為 Viola Desmond）。

English

Sir George-Étienne Cartier was the key architect of Confederation from Quebec. A railway lawyer, Montrealer, close ally of Macdonald and patriotic Canadien, Cartier led Quebec into Confederation and helped negotiate the entry of the Northwest Territories, Manitoba and British Columbia into Canada.

中文

Sir George-Étienne Cartier 是來自魁北克的邦聯關鍵推手——他是鐵路律師、蒙特婁人、Macdonald 的密友、愛國的法裔加拿大人，領 Quebec 加入邦聯，並協助談判 NWT、Manitoba、BC 加入加拿大。

Expansion of the Dominion / 自治領擴張

年份 加入的省 / 領地
1867 Ontario、Quebec、Nova Scotia、New Brunswick
1870 Manitoba、Northwest Territories
1871 British Columbia
1873 Prince Edward Island
1880 北極群島轉入 NWT
1898 Yukon Territory
1905 Alberta、Saskatchewan
1949 Newfoundland and Labrador
1999 Nunavut

Challenge in the West / 西部的挑戰

English

When Canada took over the vast northwest region from the Hudson’s Bay Company in 1869, the 12,000 Métis of the Red River were not consulted. In response, Louis Riel led an armed uprising and seized Fort Garry, the territorial capital. Canada’s future was in jeopardy. How could the Dominion reach from sea to sea if it could not control the interior?

中文

1869 年，加拿大從哈德遜灣公司接管廣大的西北地區，但 Red River 的 12,000 名梅蒂斯人未被諮詢。作為回應，Louis Riel 領導武裝起義，奪取領地首府 Fort Garry。加拿大的未來陷入危機——若無法控制內陸，何以「從海到海」？

English

Ottawa sent soldiers to retake Fort Garry in 1870. Riel fled to the United States and Canada established a new province: Manitoba. Riel was elected to Parliament but never took his seat. Later, as Métis and Indian rights were again threatened by westward settlement, a second rebellion in 1885 in present-day Saskatchewan led to Riel’s trial and execution for high treason, a decision that was strongly opposed in Quebec. Riel is seen by many as a hero, a defender of Métis rights and the father of Manitoba.

中文

1870 年渥太華派兵奪回 Fort Garry，Riel 逃往美國，加拿大建立新省 Manitoba。Riel 雖當選國會議員，卻從未就職。後來，1885 年在今 Saskatchewan 發生第二次叛亂——Riel 被審判並以叛國罪處絞，此決定在 Quebec 強烈反對。Riel 被許多人視為英雄、梅蒂斯權利的捍衛者、Manitoba 之父。

English

After the first Métis uprising, Prime Minister Macdonald established the North West Mounted Police (NWMP) in 1873 to pacify the West and assist in negotiations with the Indians. The NWMP founded Fort Calgary, Fort MacLeod and other centres that today are cities and towns. Regina became its headquarters. Today, the Royal Canadian Mounted Police (RCMP or “the Mounties”) are the national police force and one of Canada’s best-known symbols.

中文

第一次梅蒂斯叛亂後，Macdonald 總理於 1873 年成立「西北騎警 (NWMP)」 平息西部與協助與印第安人談判。NWMP 建立了 Fort Calgary、Fort MacLeod 等中心，後成為今日的城市。Regina 為其總部。今天，皇家加拿大騎警 (RCMP, “the Mounties”) 是國家警察，也是 加拿大最知名的象徵之一。

A Railway from Sea to Sea / 橫貫海岸的鐵路

English

British Columbia joined Canada in 1871 after Ottawa promised to build a railway to the West Coast. On November 7, 1885, a powerful symbol of unity was completed when Donald Smith (Lord Strathcona), the Scottish-born director of the Canadian Pacific Railway (CPR), drove the last spike. The project was financed by British and American investors and built by both European and Chinese labour. Afterwards the Chinese were subject to discrimination, including the Head Tax, a race-based entry fee. The Government of Canada apologized in 2006 for this discriminatory policy.

中文

British Columbia 於 1871 年加入加拿大，條件是渥太華 承諾興建一條通往西海岸的鐵路。1885 年 11 月 7 日，CPR（加拿大太平洋鐵路）蘇格蘭裔董事 Donald Smith（Lord Strathcona）打下最後一根道釘，象徵國家統一。工程由英美投資、歐亞勞工共建。完工後華工受到歧視，包括以族裔為基礎的「人頭稅 (Head Tax)」。加拿大政府於 2006 年正式為此歧視政策道歉。

Moving Westward / 向西發展

English

Canada’s economy grew and became more industrialized during the economic boom of the 1890s and early 1900s. One million British and one million Americans immigrated to Canada at this time.

中文

1890 年代與 1900 年代初 的繁榮使加拿大經濟成長與工業化。約 100 萬英國人與 100 萬美國人 於此期間移民加拿大。

English

Sir Wilfrid Laurier became the first French-Canadian prime minister since Confederation and encouraged immigration to the West. His portrait is on the $5 bill. The railway made it possible for immigrants, including 170,000 Ukrainians, 115,000 Poles and tens of thousands from Germany, France, Norway and Sweden to settle in the West before 1914 and develop a thriving agricultural sector.

中文

Sir Wilfrid Laurier 成為邦聯後第一位法裔加拿大籍總理，鼓勵移民到西部。他的肖像印在 $5 紙鈔上。鐵路讓 17 萬烏克蘭人、11.5 萬波蘭人、數萬德、法、挪威、瑞典人 在 1914 年前移居西部，發展出蓬勃的農業。

Women Get the Vote / 婦女取得投票權

English

At the time of Confederation, the vote was limited to property-owning adult white males. This was common in most democratic countries at the time. The effort by women to achieve the right to vote is known as the women’s suffrage movement. Its founder in Canada was Dr. Emily Stowe, the first Canadian woman to practise medicine in Canada. In 1916, Manitoba became the first province to grant voting rights to women.

中文

邦聯時期，投票權僅限於擁有財產的成年白人男性——這在當時多數民主國家很普遍。婦女爭取投票權的運動稱為「婦女參政運動 (women’s suffrage movement)」。加拿大運動的創始者是 Dr. Emily Stowe，加拿大第一位執業女醫師。1916 年，Manitoba 成為第一個給予婦女投票權的省份。

English

In 1917, thanks to the leadership of women such as Dr. Stowe and other suffragettes, the federal government of Sir Robert Borden gave women the right to vote in federal elections — first to nurses at the battle front, then to women who were related to men in active wartime service. In 1918, most Canadian female citizens aged 21 and over were granted the right to vote in federal elections. In 1921 Agnes Macphail, a farmer and teacher, became the first woman MP. Due to the work of Thérèse Casgrain and others, Quebec granted women the vote in 1940.

中文

1917 年，得益於 Dr. Stowe 與其他婦女領袖，Sir Robert Borden 政府 賦予婦女聯邦投票權——先給前線護士，再給戰時服役男性的女眷。1918 年，多數 21 歲以上加拿大女性公民取得聯邦投票權。1921 年，農民、教師 Agnes Macphail 成為首位女性 MP。由於 Thérèse Casgrain 等人的努力，Quebec 於 1940 年才賦予婦女投票權。

The First World War / 第一次世界大戰

English

Most Canadians were proud to be part of the British Empire. Over 7,000 volunteered to fight in the South African War (1899–1902), popularly known as the Boer War, and over 260 died.

中文

多數加拿大人 以身為大英帝國一員為傲。逾 7,000 人志願參加 1899–1902 年波耳戰爭 (Boer War)，260 餘人陣亡。

English

When Germany attacked Belgium and France in 1914 and Britain declared war, Ottawa formed the Canadian Expeditionary Force (later the Canadian Corps). More than 600,000 Canadians served in the war, most of them volunteers, out of a total population of eight million.

中文

1914 年德國攻擊比利時與法國，英國宣戰，渥太華組建「加拿大遠征軍 (CEF)」（後稱「加拿大軍團」）。在 800 萬總人口中，逾 60 萬加拿大人服役，多為志願軍。

English

On the battlefield, the Canadians proved to be tough, innovative soldiers. The Canadian Corps captured Vimy Ridge in April 1917, with 10,000 killed or wounded, securing the Canadians’ reputation for valour as the “shock troops of the British Empire.” One Canadian officer said: “It was Canada from the Atlantic to the Pacific on parade … In those few minutes I witnessed the birth of a nation.” April 9 is celebrated as Vimy Day.

中文

戰場上加拿大人證明自己 堅強、創新。1917 年 4 月加拿大軍團攻下 Vimy Ridge（10,000 人傷亡），確立 「大英帝國衝擊部隊」 的英勇美譽。一位加拿大軍官說：「那是從大西洋到太平洋的整個加拿大在閱兵……短短幾分鐘內，我見證了一個國家的誕生。」4 月 9 日被定為 Vimy Day。

English

Regrettably, from 1914 to 1920, Ottawa interned over 8,000 former Austro-Hungarian subjects, mainly Ukrainian men, as “enemy aliens” in 24 labour camps across Canada, even though Britain advised against the policy.

中文

1914–1920 年，渥太華將逾 8,000 名前奧匈帝國臣民（多為烏克蘭男性）以「敵國僑民」名義關進全國 24 個勞改營——即使英國建議不要如此。

English

In 1918, under the command of General Sir Arthur Currie, Canada’s greatest soldier, the Canadian Corps advanced alongside the French and British Empire troops in the last hundred days. These included the victorious Battle of Amiens on August 8, 1918–which the Germans called “the black day of the German Army”–followed by Arras, Canal du Nord, Cambrai and Mons. With Germany and Austria’s surrender, the war ended in the Armistice on November 11, 1918. In total 60,000 Canadians were killed and 170,000 wounded.

中文

1918 年，加拿大史上最偉大的軍人 General Sir Arthur Currie 指揮加拿大軍團與英法部隊在最後一百日進攻。包括 1918 年 8 月 8 日勝利的阿米恩斯戰役 (Battle of Amiens)——德國人稱之為 「德軍的黑色之日」——以及隨後的 Arras、Canal du Nord、Cambrai、Mons。德奧投降，戰爭於 1918 年 11 月 11 日停戰 (Armistice) 結束。共有 60,000 名加拿大人陣亡、170,000 人受傷。

Remembrance Day / 國殤紀念日

English

Canadians remember the sacrifices of our veterans and brave fallen in all wars up to the present day in which Canadians took part, each year on November 11: Remembrance Day. Canadians wear the red poppy and observe a moment of silence at the 11th hour of the 11th day of the 11th month to honour the sacrifices of over a million brave men and women who have served, and the 110,000 who have given their lives.

中文

每年 11 月 11 日國殤紀念日 (Remembrance Day)，加拿大人紀念至今所有戰爭中犧牲的退伍軍人與烈士。加拿大人 佩戴紅罌粟花 (red poppy)，於 11 月 11 日 11 時 默哀片刻，以紀念逾百萬服役者與 11 萬陣亡者的犧牲。

English

Canadian medical officer Lieutenant-Colonel John McCrae composed the poem “In Flanders Fields” in 1915.

中文

加拿大軍醫中校 John McCrae 於 1915 年寫下《In Flanders Fields》詩篇。

In Flanders Fields（節選翻譯）

English

In Flanders fields the poppies blow / Between the crosses, row on row, / That mark our place; and in the sky / The larks, still bravely singing, fly / Scarce heard amid the guns below.

中文

弗蘭德斯野上罌粟花飄揚， 在我們長眠的十字架間排成行。 天上雲雀仍勇敢地歌唱， 但在底下隆隆的砲火中幾不可聞。

English

We are the dead. Short days ago / We lived, felt dawn, saw sunset glow, / Loved, and were loved, and now we lie / In Flanders fields.

中文

我們已是亡者。不久之前， 我們也曾活著，感受清晨、看見落日， 愛人，被愛，如今卻長眠 於弗蘭德斯野。

English

Take up our quarrel with the foe: / To you from failing hands we throw / The torch; be yours to hold it high. / If ye break faith with us who die / We shall not sleep, though poppies grow / In Flanders fields.

中文

接過我們與敵人的戰鬥： 我們從垂落的手中將火炬擲予你， 請你高舉。若你背棄了我們這些死者的信約， 即使罌粟花在弗蘭德斯野盛開， 我們也將不得安眠。

Between the Wars / 戰間期

English

After the First World War, the British Empire evolved into a free association of states known as the British Commonwealth of Nations. Canada remains a leading member of the Commonwealth to this day, together with other successor states of the Empire such as India, Australia, New Zealand, and several African and Caribbean countries.

中文

一戰後，大英帝國演變為 「大英國協 (British Commonwealth of Nations)」——一個自由結合的國家組織。加拿大至今仍是國協的主要成員，與印度、澳洲、紐西蘭等繼承國一同。

English

The “Roaring Twenties” were boom times, with prosperity for businesses and low unemployment. The stock market crash of 1929, however, led to the Great Depression or the “Dirty Thirties.” Unemployment reached 27% in 1933 and many businesses were wiped out. Farmers in Western Canada were hit hardest by low grain prices and a terrible drought.

中文

「咆哮的二十年代 (Roaring Twenties)」是繁榮期，企業興旺、失業率低。1929 年股市崩盤導致大蕭條（「骯髒的三十年代」）。1933 年失業率達 27%，大量企業倒閉。西部農民因穀價低與大旱受創最重。

English

There was growing demand for the government to create a social safety net with minimum wages, a standard work week and programs such as unemployment insurance. The Bank of Canada, a central bank to manage the money supply and bring stability to the financial system, was created in 1934.

中文

愈來愈多人要求政府建立 社會安全網：最低工資、標準工時、失業保險。1934 年加拿大銀行 (Bank of Canada)——中央銀行——成立。

The Second World War / 第二次世界大戰

English

The Second World War began in 1939 when Adolf Hitler, the National Socialist (Nazi) dictator of Germany, invaded Poland and conquered much of Europe. Canada joined with its democratic allies in the fight to defeat tyranny by force of arms.

中文

1939 年，德國納粹獨裁者希特勒入侵波蘭並征服大部分歐洲，二戰爆發。加拿大與其民主盟邦一同參戰。

English

More than one million Canadians and Newfoundlanders (Newfoundland was a separate British entity) served in the Second World War, out of a population of 11.5 million. This was a high proportion and of these, 44,000 were killed.

中文

1,150 萬人口中，逾 100 萬加拿大人與紐芬蘭人服役（當時 Newfoundland 尚為獨立的英屬實體）。其中 44,000 人陣亡。

English

The Canadians fought bravely and suffered losses in the unsuccessful defence of Hong Kong (1941) from attack by Imperial Japan, and in a failed raid on Nazi-controlled Dieppe on the coast of France (1942).

中文

加拿大人英勇奮戰，於 1941 年香港防衛戰 對抗日本帝國、1942 年 Dieppe 突襲（失敗） 中蒙受重大損失。

English

The Royal Canadian Air Force (RCAF) took part in the Battle of Britain and provided a high proportion of Commonwealth aircrew in bombers and fighter planes over Europe. Moreover, Canada contributed more to the Allied air effort than any other Commonwealth country, with over 130,000 Allied air crew trained in Canada under the British Commonwealth Air Training Plan.

中文

皇家加拿大空軍 (RCAF) 參與不列顛戰役，並提供大量盟邦空勤人員。加拿大對盟軍空戰的貢獻超過任何其他國協國家——在 「英聯空中訓練計劃」 下於加拿大訓練超過 130,000 名盟軍空勤。

English

The Royal Canadian Navy (RCN) saw its finest hour in the Battle of the Atlantic, protecting convoys of merchant ships against German submarines. Canada’s Merchant Navy helped to feed, clothe and resupply Britain. At the end of the Second World War, Canada had the third-largest navy in the world.

中文

皇家加拿大海軍 (RCN) 的巔峰時刻是大西洋戰役——保護商船船團對抗德軍潛艇。加拿大商船隊協助餵養、補給英國。二戰結束時，加拿大擁有世界第三大海軍。

English

In the Pacific war, Japan invaded the Aleutian Islands, attacked a lighthouse on Vancouver Island, launched fire balloons over B.C. and the Prairies, and grossly maltreated Canadian prisoners of war captured at Hong Kong. Japan surrendered on August 14, 1945—the end of four years of war in the Pacific.

中文

太平洋戰場中，日本入侵阿留申群島、攻擊溫哥華島燈塔、向 BC 與草原省發射火氣球，並嚴重虐待香港被俘的加軍戰俘。日本於 1945 年 8 月 14 日投降——太平洋戰爭結束。

English

Regrettably, the state of war and public opinion in B.C. led to the forcible relocation of Canadians of Japanese origin by the federal government and the sale of their property without compensation. This occurred even though the military and the RCMP told Ottawa that they posed little danger to Canada. The Government of Canada apologized in 1988 for wartime wrongs and compensated the victims.

中文

戰爭狀態與 BC 輿論導致 聯邦政府強制遷離日裔加拿大人，且未經補償變賣其財產——儘管軍方與 RCMP 告知渥太華他們對加拿大幾無威脅。加拿大政府於 1988 年正式為戰時錯誤道歉並補償受害者。

D-Day / D 日

English

In order to defeat Nazism and Fascism, the Allies invaded Nazi-occupied Europe. Canadians took part in the liberation of Italy in 1943–44. In the epic invasion of Normandy in northern France on June 6, 1944, known as D-Day, 15,000 Canadian troops stormed and captured Juno Beach from the German Army. Approximately one in ten Allied soldiers on D-Day was Canadian. The Canadian Army liberated the Netherlands in 1944–45 and helped force the German surrender of May 8, 1945, bringing to an end six years of war in Europe.

中文

為了擊敗納粹與法西斯主義，盟軍進攻納粹佔領下的歐洲。加拿大人於 1943–44 年參與義大利的解放。1944 年 6 月 6 日諾曼第大登陸（D-Day），15,000 名加拿大士兵衝鋒佔領 Juno Beach。D-Day 當日每 10 名盟軍士兵就有 1 名是加拿大人。加拿大軍於 1944–45 年解放荷蘭，並協助迫使 1945 年 5 月 8 日德國投降，結束了六年的歐洲戰爭。

第 5 章：現代加拿大 / Modern Canada

對應 PDF 第 24–27 頁。對應 day-06.md。

A Changing Society / 變遷中的社會

English

As social values changed over more than 50 years, Canada became a more flexible and open society. Many took advantage of expanding secondary and postsecondary educational opportunities and a growing number of women entered the professional work force.

中文

過去 50 多年來，社會價值觀變化，加拿大變得更具彈性、更開放。許多人受惠於 擴大的中學與高等教育機會，越來越多女性進入專業職場。

English

Most Canadians of Asian descent had in the past been denied the vote in federal and provincial elections. In 1948 the last of these, the Japanese-Canadians, gained the right to vote. Aboriginal people were granted the vote in 1960. Today every citizen over the age of 18 may vote.

中文

過去 多數亞裔加拿大人被剝奪聯邦與省級投票權。1948 年，最後一批——日裔加拿大人——取得投票權。1960 年，原住民取得投票權。今日 18 歲以上的每位公民都可投票。

English

Canada welcomed thousands of refugees from Communist oppression, including about 37,000 who escaped Soviet tyranny in Hungary in 1956. With the Communist victory in the Vietnam War in 1975, many Vietnamese fled, including over 50,000 who sought refuge in Canada.

中文

加拿大接納了數千名逃離共產暴政的難民，包括 1956 年從蘇聯壓迫下逃出的約 37,000 名匈牙利人。1975 年越戰共產勝利後，越南難民逃亡，逾 50,000 人到加拿大尋求庇護。

English

The idea of multiculturalism, as a result of 19th- and 20th-century immigration, gained a new impetus. By the 1960s, one-third of Canadians had origins that were neither British nor French, and took pride in preserving their distinct culture in the Canadian fabric. Today, diversity enriches Canadians’ lives, particularly in our cities.

中文

「多元文化主義」 因 19、20 世紀移民而獲得新動力。1960 年代，三分之一加拿大人的祖源既非英也非法，他們以在加拿大社會中保有自身獨特文化為榮。今日 多元豐富了加拿大人的生活，尤其在都市。

Trade and Economic Growth / 貿易與經濟成長

English

Postwar Canada enjoyed record prosperity and material progress. The world’s restrictive trading policies in the Depression era were opened up by such treaties as the General Agreement on Tariffs and Trade (GATT), now the World Trade Organization (WTO).

中文

戰後加拿大享有 空前繁榮與物質進步。大蕭條時期的貿易限制透過 《關稅暨貿易總協定》(GATT)（即今 世界貿易組織 WTO）逐步開放。

English

The discovery of oil in Alberta in 1947 began Canada’s modern energy industry. In 1951, for the first time, a majority of Canadians were able to afford adequate food, shelter and clothing. Between 1945 and 1970, as Canada drew closer to the United States and other trading partners, the country enjoyed one of the strongest economies among industrialized nations. Today, Canadians enjoy one of the world’s highest standards of living—maintained by the hard work of Canadians and by trade with other nations, in particular the United States.

中文

1947 年 Alberta 發現石油 開啟加拿大現代能源產業。1951 年，多數加拿大人首次能負擔充足的食、住、衣。1945–1970 年間，加拿大與美國及其他貿易夥伴關係日深，成為工業國家中經濟最強的之一。今日加拿大人享有 世界最高生活水準之一——靠的是加拿大人的勤奮 與對外貿易（尤其與美國）。

English

As prosperity grew, so did the ability to support social assistance programs. The Canada Health Act ensures common elements and a basic standard of coverage. Unemployment insurance (now called “employment insurance”) was introduced by the federal government in 1940. Old Age Security was devised as early as 1927, and the Canada and Quebec Pension Plans in 1965. Publicly funded education is provided by the provinces and territories.

中文

繁榮增長也帶來社會福利的擴張。《加拿大健康法 (Canada Health Act)》確保健保的共通要素與基本給付。失業保險（現稱「就業保險」）由聯邦於 1940 年引入；老人福利金 (Old Age Security) 早在 1927 年就規劃；加拿大與魁北克退休金 (CPP/QPP) 於 1965 年實施。公共教育由省／領地提供。

International Engagement / 國際參與

English

Like Australia, New Zealand and other countries, Canada developed its autonomy gradually with a capacity to make significant contributions internationally.

中文

如同澳洲、紐西蘭等國，加拿大 逐步發展自治，並具備對國際事務的重要貢獻能力。

English

The Cold War began when several liberated countries of eastern Europe became part of a Communist bloc controlled by the Soviet Union under the dictator Josef Stalin. Canada joined with other democratic countries of the West to form the North Atlantic Treaty Organization (NATO), a military alliance, and with the United States in the North American Aerospace Defence Command (NORAD).

中文

冷戰始於 東歐多國被史達林獨裁的蘇聯併入共產集團。加拿大與西方民主國家共組 北大西洋公約組織 (NATO) 軍事同盟，並與美國共組 北美防空司令部 (NORAD)。

English

Canada joined international organizations such as the United Nations (UN). It participated in the UN operation defending South Korea in the Korean War (1950–53), with 500 dead and 1,000 wounded. Canada has taken part in numerous UN peacekeeping missions in places as varied as Egypt, Cyprus and Haiti, as well as in other international security operations such as those in the former Yugoslavia and Afghanistan.

中文

加拿大加入 聯合國 (UN) 等國際組織。參與韓戰 (1950–53) 保衛南韓的聯合國行動，陣亡 500 人，受傷 1,000 人。加拿大也參與 埃及、塞普勒斯、海地等聯合國維和任務，以及 前南斯拉夫、阿富汗 等國際安全行動。

Canada and Quebec / 加拿大與魁北克

English

French-Canadian society and culture flourished in the postwar years. Quebec experienced an era of rapid change in the 1960s known as the Quiet Revolution. Many Quebecers sought to separate from Canada. In 1963 Parliament established the Royal Commission on Bilingualism and Biculturalism. This led to the Official Languages Act (1969), which guarantees French and English services in the federal government across Canada. In 1970, Canada helped found La Francophonie, an international association of French-speaking countries.

中文

戰後法裔加拿大社會與文化蓬勃發展。1960 年代魁北克經歷劇變，史稱「寧靜革命 (Quiet Revolution)」。許多魁北克人尋求脫離加拿大獨立。1963 年國會成立「皇家雙語雙文化委員會」，最終導致 1969 年《官方語言法 (Official Languages Act)》——確保聯邦政府在全國以法、英兩語提供服務。1970 年加拿大協助創立「法語國家組織 (La Francophonie)」——一個法語國家國際組織。

English

The movement for Quebec sovereignty gained strength but was defeated in a referendum in the province in 1980. After much negotiation, in 1982 the Constitution was amended without the agreement of Quebec. Though sovereignty was again defeated in a second referendum in 1995, the autonomy of Quebec within Canada remains a lively topic—part of the dynamic that continues to shape our country.

中文

魁北克獨立運動勢力高漲，但 1980 年首次公投失敗。經多番談判後，1982 年憲法修正案未獲魁北克同意通過。1995 年第二次公投獨立再次失敗，但魁北克在加拿大內的自治仍是熱門話題，持續形塑著國家。

Arts and Culture / 藝術與文化

English

Canadian artists have a long history of achievement in which Canadians take pride. Artists from all regions reflect and define our culture and forms of creative expression and have achieved greatness both at home and abroad.

中文

加拿大藝術家有令國人自豪的傳統。來自各地的藝術家 反映並定義了我們的文化，在國內外皆成就斐然。

English

Canadians have made significant contributions to literature in English and in French. Novelists, poets, historians, educators and musicians have had a significant cultural impact. Men and women of letters included Stephen Leacock, Louis Hémon, Sir Charles G.D. Roberts, Pauline Johnson, Émile Nelligan, Robertson Davies, Margaret Laurence and Mordecai Richler. Musicians such as Sir Ernest MacMillan and Healey Willan won renown in Canada and abroad. Writers such as Joy Kogawa, Michael Ondaatje and Rohinton Mistry have diversified Canada’s literary experience.

中文

加拿大人在 英文與法文文學 都有重要貢獻：小說家、詩人、史學家、教育家與音樂家影響深遠。文學家包括 Stephen Leacock、Louis Hémon、Pauline Johnson、Robertson Davies、Margaret Laurence、Mordecai Richler。音樂家如 Sir Ernest MacMillan 享譽國內外。Joy Kogawa、Michael Ondaatje、Rohinton Mistry 等作家豐富了加拿大文學的多元面貌。

English

In the visual arts, Canada is historically perhaps best known for the Group of Seven, founded in 1920, who developed a style of painting to capture the rugged wilderness landscapes. Emily Carr painted the forests and Aboriginal artifacts of the West Coast. Les Automatistes of Quebec were pioneers of modern abstract art in the 1950s, most notably Jean-Paul Riopelle. Quebec’s Louis-Philippe Hébert was a celebrated sculptor of historical figures. Kenojuak Ashevak pioneered modern Inuit art with etchings, prints and soapstone sculptures.

中文

視覺藝術上，加拿大最知名的是 1920 年成立的「七人團 (Group of Seven)」——他們發展出一種捕捉荒野風景的繪畫風格。Emily Carr 描繪西海岸森林與原住民文物。1950 年代魁北克的 Les Automatistes 是現代抽象藝術的先驅，最著名為 Jean-Paul Riopelle。Kenojuak Ashevak 以蝕刻、版畫與皂石雕刻開創現代因紐特藝術。

English

Canada has a long and respected performing arts history, with a network of regional theatres and world-renowned performing arts companies.

The films of Denys Arcand have been popular in Quebec and across the country, and have won international awards. Other noteworthy Canadian filmmakers include Norman Jewison and Atom Egoyan. Canadian television has had a popular following.

中文

加拿大有悠久的表演藝術歷史，擁有 遍布全國的劇院網絡 與 世界知名表演團體。Denys Arcand 的電影在魁北克與全國廣受歡迎並獲國際獎項；其他著名導演包括 Norman Jewison 與 Atom Egoyan。加拿大電視亦深受歡迎。

Sports / 運動

English

Sports have flourished as all provinces and territories have produced amateur and professional star athletes and Olympic medal winners. Basketball was invented by Canadian James Naismith in 1891. Many major league sports boast Canadian talent and in the national sport of ice hockey, Canadian teams have dominated the world. In 1996 at the Olympic Summer Games, Donovan Bailey became a world record sprinter and double Olympic gold medallist. Chantal Petitclerc became a world champion wheelchair racer and Paralympic gold medalist. One of the greatest hockey players of all time, Wayne Gretzky, played for the Edmonton Oilers from 1979 to 1988.

中文

運動發展蓬勃，各省/領地都產出業餘與職業明星運動員及奧運獎牌得主。籃球由加拿大人 James Naismith 於 1891 年發明。許多主要聯盟運動有加拿大人才；在 冰球（國民運動） 中加拿大隊稱霸世界。1996 年奧運，Donovan Bailey 成為世界紀錄短跑選手、雙金牌。Chantal Petitclerc 是輪椅競速世界冠軍、帕運金牌。史上最偉大冰球選手之一 Wayne Gretzky 於 1979–1988 年效力 Edmonton Oilers。

English

In 1980, Terry Fox, a British Columbian who lost his right leg to cancer at the age of 18, began a cross-country run, the “Marathon of Hope,” to raise money for cancer research. He became a hero to Canadians. While he did not finish the run and ultimately lost his battle with cancer, his legacy continues through yearly fundraising events in his name. In 1985, fellow British Columbian Rick Hansen circled the globe in a wheelchair to raise funds for spinal cord research.

中文

1980 年，18 歲就因癌症失去右腿的 BC 青年 Terry Fox，開始橫越加拿大的 「希望馬拉松 (Marathon of Hope)」 為癌症研究募款。他成為加拿大人的英雄。他雖未完成跑步、最終敗給癌症，但每年以他名義的募款活動延續他的精神。1985 年，同樣來自 BC 的 Rick Hansen 以輪椅環球募款，支持脊髓研究。

Great Canadian Discoveries and Inventions / 加拿大重大發現與發明

English

Canadians have made various discoveries and inventions. Some of the most famous are listed below:

中文
人物 貢獻
Alexander Graham Bell 在加拿大避暑屋構思 電話 (telephone)
Joseph-Armand Bombardier 發明 雪地摩托車 (snowmobile)
Sir Sandford Fleming 發明全球 標準時區 (standard time zones)
Matthew Evans & Henry Woodward 共同發明 第一顆電燈泡，後將專利賣給 Thomas Edison
Reginald Fessenden 對 廣播 (radio) 的發明有貢獻，發出世界首則無線語音訊息
Dr. Wilder Penfield McGill 大學腦神經外科先驅，曾被譽為「最偉大的在世加拿大人」
Dr. John A. Hopps 發明 第一台心律調節器 (cardiac pacemaker)
SPAR Aerospace / 國家研究院 發明 Canadarm——太空用機械手臂
Mike Lazaridis 與 Jim Balsillie（RIM 公司） 發明 黑莓機 (BlackBerry)
Sir Frederick Banting 與 Charles Best 在 Toronto 發現 胰島素 (insulin)，救了全球 1,600 萬糖尿病患者
English

Canadian advances in science and technology are world renowned and have changed the way the world communicates and does business. Marshall McLuhan and Harold Innis were pioneer thinkers. Science and research in Canada have won international recognition and attracted world-class students, academics and entrepreneurs engaged in medical research, telecommunications and other fields. Since 1989, the Canadian Space Agency and Canadian astronauts have participated in space exploration, often using the Canadian-designed and built Canadarm.

中文

加拿大在科技上的成就舉世聞名，改變了全球通訊與商業方式。Marshall McLuhan 與 Harold Innis 是先驅思想家。1989 年起加拿大太空總署 (CSA) 與加籍太空人參與太空探索，常使用加拿大設計與製造的 Canadarm。

English

Gerhard Herzberg, a refugee from Nazi Germany, John Polanyi, Sidney Altman, Richard E. Taylor, Michael Smith and Bertram Brockhouse were Nobel Prize-winning scientists.

中文

Gerhard Herzberg（納粹德國難民）、John Polanyi、Sidney Altman、Richard E. Taylor、Michael Smith、Bertram Brockhouse 都是諾貝爾獎科學家。

結語 / Closing

English

The prosperity and diversity of our country depend on all Canadians working together to face challenges of the future. In seeking to become a citizen, you are joining a country that, with your active participation, will continue to grow and thrive.

中文

加拿大的繁榮與多元，仰賴所有加拿大人共同面對未來挑戰。在尋求成為公民的路上，您正加入一個有您積極參與、將會持續茁壯的國家。

English

How will you make your contribution to Canada?

中文

您會如何為加拿大做出貢獻？
